        {
            "id": "16715fc0-c23f-4941-887b-34882a594fdf",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {
                    "real_author": "tool:web"
                }
            },
            "create_time": 1792070588.851405,
            "update_time": 1792070593.669932,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "For **Elite X, Sector 10, Greater Noida West**, I found a publicly listed payment schedule, but the developer's official website does not publish a complete payment plan that I could independently verify. So treat the schedule below as a plan to confirm with the sales team, not a binding offer.\n\n## 1. Payment plan listed online\n\nThe third-party Elite X listing shows this schedule: <Cite refs={[\"turn238721search0\"]}/>\n\n| Payment milestone | Percentage | On a ₹2.10 Cr base price |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| At booking | 10% | ₹21 lakh |\n| Within 45 days of booking | 40% | ₹84 lakh |\n| On completion of structure | 20% | ₹42 lakh |\n| On external painting of tower | 20% | ₹42 lakh |\n| **Total listed** | **90%** | **₹1.89 crore** |\n| Remaining amount | **10%** | **₹21 lakh** |\n\nThe published schedule accounts for only 90% of the price. It does not clearly identify the milestone for the remaining 10%, so get that confirmed in writing.\n\n**My main concern:** you may be asked to pay 50% of the base price, or ₹1.05 crore, within just 45 days. That is a substantial upfront commitment for a project still under construction.\n\n## 2. What you should ask the builder\n\nAsk for these three documents before paying a booking amount:\n\n- The latest official payment plan, including the missing final 10%.\n- The complete unit-wise cost sheet, including parking, GST if applicable, registration and other charges.\n- The agreement for sale showing that construction-linked payments correspond to verifiable milestones.\n\nElite X's official website lists its sales contact as **+91 92133 31222** and **enquiry@elitegroup.co**. You can request the current payment plan directly from the developer. <Cite refs={[\"turn238721search2\"]}/>\n\n- [Official Elite Group contact page](https://elitegroup.co/contact/)\n- [UP-RERA project record](https://www.up-rera.in/)\n\n## 3. Which payment plan would I choose?\n\nGiven that you need a home within two years, I would favour a **construction-linked plan** over a plan that requires large payments based primarily on fixed dates.\n\nIf the builder offers a 10:40:20:20:10 schedule, I would specifically negotiate the 40% payment due within 45 days. Ask whether it can be divided into verified construction milestones instead.\n\nDo not assume the builder will agree, and do not rely on a salesperson's verbal promise. Any revised schedule must be recorded in the agreement.\n\nOne final point: Elite X's registered possession deadline is **12 December 2028**. If your two-year home deadline is firm, check whether the current construction progress and the contractual possession date give you enough certainty before committing. <Cite refs={[\"turn238721search4\"]}/>\n\n"
                ]
            },
            "status": "finished_successfully",

                    "constants": {
                        "0": "For ",
                        "1": "Elite X, Sector 10, Greater Noida West",
                        "2": ", I found a publicly listed payment schedule, but the developer's official website does not publish a complete payment plan that I could independently verify. So treat the schedule below as a plan to confirm with the sales team, not a binding offer.",
                        "3": "1. Payment plan listed online",
                        "4": "The third-party Elite X listing shows this schedule: ",
                        "5": [
                            "turn238721search0"
                        ],
                        "6": "624eac0f-78cb-533b-95ff-98c4c916401a",
                        "7": "Payment milestone",
                        "8": "Percentage",
                        "9": "On a ₹2.10 Cr base price",
                        "10": "At booking",
                        "11": "10%",
                        "12": "₹21 lakh",
                        "13": "Within 45 days of booking",
                        "14": "40%",
                        "15": "₹84 lakh",
                        "16": "On completion of structure",
                        "17": "20%",
                        "18": "₹42 lakh",
                        "19": "On external painting of tower",
                        "20": "20%",
                        "21": "₹42 lakh",
                        "22": "Total listed",
                        "23": "90%",
                        "24": "₹1.89 crore",
                        "25": "Remaining amount",
                        "26": "10%",
                        "27": "₹21 lakh",
                        "28": "The published schedule accounts for only 90% of the price. It does not clearly identify the milestone for the remaining 10%, so get that confirmed in writing.",
                        "29": "My main concern:",
                        "30": " you may be asked to pay 50% of the base price, or ₹1.05 crore, within just 45 days. That is a substantial upfront commitment for a project still under construction.",
                        "31": "2. What you should ask the builder",
                        "32": "Ask for these three documents before paying a booking amount:",
                        "33": "The latest official payment plan, including the missing final 10%.",
                        "34": "The complete unit-wise cost sheet, including parking, GST if applicable, registration and other charges.",
                        "35": "The agreement for sale showing that construction-linked payments correspond to verifiable milestones.",
                        "36": "Elite X's official website lists its sales contact as ",
                        "37": "+91 92133 31222",
                        "38": " and ",
                        "39": "enquiry@elitegroup.co",
                        "40": ". You can request the current payment plan directly from the developer. ",
                        "41": [
                            "turn238721search2"
                        ],
                        "42": "942d2c4a-6ecd-545a-a687-b8420b25b9e3",
                        "43": "25bfac6e-fbcd-5e68-a028-1e13eb99de53",
                        "44": "Official Elite Group contact page",
                        "45": "dedf923e-efb9-5796-821d-1bd07d4a9183",
                        "46": "UP-RERA project record",
                        "47": "3. Which payment plan would I choose?",
                        "48": "Given that you need a home within two years, I would favour a ",
                        "49": "construction-linked plan",
                        "50": " over a plan that requires large payments based primarily on fixed dates.",
                        "51": "If the builder offers a 10:40:20:20:10 schedule, I would specifically negotiate the 40% payment due within 45 days. Ask whether it can be divided into verified construction milestones instead.",
                        "52": "Do not assume the builder will agree, and do not rely on a salesperson's verbal promise. Any revised schedule must be recorded in the agreement.",
                        "53": "One final point: Elite X's registered possession deadline is ",
                        "54": "12 December 2028",
                        "55": ". If your two-year home deadline is firm, check whether the current construction progress and the contractual possession date give you enough certainty before committing. ",
                        "56": [
                            "turn238721search4"
                        ],
                        "57": "965e8727-dd6e-5984-99ee-d31eb3b63259"
                    },
                    "fallbackMarkdown": "For **Elite X, Sector 10, Greater Noida West**, I found a publicly listed payment schedule, but the developer's official website does not publish a complete payment plan that I could independently verify. So treat the schedule below as a plan to confirm with the sales team, not a binding offer.\n\n## 1\\. Payment plan listed online\n\nThe third-party Elite X listing shows this schedule: citeturn238721search0\n\n| Payment milestone | Percentage | On a ₹2.10 Cr base price |\n|--------|--------|--------|\n| At booking | 10% | ₹21 lakh |\n| Within 45 days of booking | 40% | ₹84 lakh |\n| On completion of structure | 20% | ₹42 lakh |\n| On external painting of tower | 20% | ₹42 lakh |\n| **Total listed** | **90%** | **₹1.89 crore** |\n| Remaining amount | **10%** | **₹21 lakh** |\n\nThe published schedule accounts for only 90% of the price. It does not clearly identify the milestone for the remaining 10%, so get that confirmed in writing.\n\n**My main concern\\:** you may be asked to pay 50% of the base price, or ₹1.05 crore, within just 45 days. That is a substantial upfront commitment for a project still under construction.\n\n## 2\\. What you should ask the builder\n\nAsk for these three documents before paying a booking amount\\:\n\n- The latest official payment plan, including the missing final 10%.\n- The complete unit-wise cost sheet, including parking, GST if applicable, registration and other charges.\n- The agreement for sale showing that construction-linked payments correspond to verifiable milestones.\n\nElite X's official website lists its sales contact as **\\+91 92133 31222** and **enquiry\\@elitegroup.co**\\. You can request the current payment plan directly from the developer. citeturn238721search2\n\n- urlOfficial Elite Group contact pagehttps://elitegroup.co/contact/\n- urlUP-RERA project recordhttps://www.up-rera.in/\n\n## 3\\. Which payment plan would I choose?\n\nGiven that you need a home within two years, I would favour a **construction-linked plan** over a plan that requires large payments based primarily on fixed dates.\n\nIf the builder offers a 10:40:20:20:10 schedule, I would specifically negotiate the 40% payment due within 45 days. Ask whether it can be divided into verified construction milestones instead.\n\nDo not assume the builder will agree, and do not rely on a salesperson's verbal promise. Any revised schedule must be recorded in the agreement.\n\nOne final point: Elite X's registered possession deadline is **12 December 2028**\\. If your two-year home deadline is firm, check whether the current construction progress and the contractual possession date give you enough certainty before committing. citeturn238721search4",

        {
            "id": "9d088811-c41c-4264-89a7-f4a683eede0a",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {}
            },
            "create_time": 1792070609.044211,
            "update_time": 1792070619.622985,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "For your everyday life, I would shortlist **Nimbus The Hyde Park in Sector 78 first, followed by Sethi Max Royal in Sector 76 and Arihant Abode in Sector 10, Greater Noida West**.\n\nI'm basing this on your actual priorities: a 3BHK for your family, a budget of ₹2 crore, good schools nearby, plenty of open space, shopping and daily conveniences, a reasonable commute to Sector 63, and a home you can move into within two years.\n\nThe key decision is whether you value Central Noida's established connectivity more than Greater Noida West's newer developments and potentially larger apartment for the money.\n\n## My shortlist for your everyday life\n\n<MapWidgetV2 title=\"Societies worth visiting\" points={[{\"ref\":\"turn558351business10\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business0\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business12\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business4\"}]} carousel={{\"visibility\":\"hidden\"}}/>\n\n<box gap={4}>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <AsyncImage query=\"IITL Nimbus The Hyde Park Sector 78 Noida landscaped central park apartment towers\" aspectRatio=\"4:5\" maxWidth=\"124px\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      <title size=\"lg\">1. <Entity ref=\"turn558351business10\" value=\"Nimbus The Hyde Park\" category=\"local_business\"/></title>\n      <badge color=\"success\">Best overall fit</badge>\n      <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Sector 78 · Established society</text>\n      This is my first choice for your family because the campus has landscaped open areas, children's play spaces and amenities, while Sector 78 has established shopping, schools and metro access. <Cite refs={[\"turn558351search0\",\"turn558351search8\"]}/>\n\n      **Budget:** Recent listings show some 3BHK resale apartments around ₹1.6 crore, leaving more room for transaction costs than a ₹2 crore purchase. <Cite refs={[\"turn558351search0\"]}/>\n\n      **Check carefully:** Residents' reviews raise concerns about seepage, interior finishes and maintenance in some apartments. Inspect the exact tower and flat rather than assuming quality is uniform.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <AsyncImage query=\"Sethi Max Royal Sector 76 Noida residential society landscaped courtyard towers\" aspectRatio=\"4:5\" maxWidth=\"124px\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      <title size=\"lg\">2. <Entity ref=\"turn558351business0\" value=\"Sethi Max Royal\" category=\"local_business\"/></title>\n      <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Sector 76 · Connectivity-focused choice</text>\n      A good alternative if your daily commute and access to public transport matter more than having the largest landscaped campus.\n\n      **Why it fits:** Established gated housing, amenities and proximity to Sector 76 metro.\n\n      **Watch out for:** Traffic around the entrance, waterlogging or seepage history, and whether the available 3BHK has enough usable space for your family.\n\n      **Budget:** Recent resale listings have shown apartments around ₹1.6 crore, though the exact unit and total acquisition cost need confirmation. <Cite refs={[\"turn558351search0\",\"turn558351business0\"]}/>\n    </box>\n  </row>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <AsyncImage query=\"Arihant Abode Sector 10 Greater Noida West residential society landscaped green courtyard towers\" aspectRatio=\"4:5\" maxWidth=\"124px\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      <title size=\"lg\">3. <Entity ref=\"turn558351business12\" value=\"Arihant Abode\" category=\"local_business\"/></title>\n      <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Sector 10, Greater Noida West · Local alternative</text>\n      Keep this on your list if you prefer staying in Greater Noida West and want a newer-style, amenity-focused residential community.\n\n      **Why it fits:** Family facilities, a clubhouse and a residential setting within your preferred broader area.\n\n      **Watch out for:** Check the exact apartment's sunlight, ventilation, water quality, maintenance charges and usable area. Do not assume that a large advertised super area means a large living space.\n\n      **Budget:** Verify the current ready-to-move resale inventory and registry eligibility before comparing it with Central Noida.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <AsyncImage query=\"Sunshine Helios Sector 78 Noida residential apartment society garden towers\" aspectRatio=\"4:5\" maxWidth=\"124px\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      <title size=\"lg\">4. <Entity ref=\"turn558351business4\" value=\"Sunshine Helios\" category=\"local_business\"/></title>\n      <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Sector 78 · Alternative to compare</text>\n      Add this to your visits if you want another established society in the same general residential belt. Compare the actual 3BHK layout, light, ventilation and maintenance against The Hyde Park.\n\n      **Why it fits:** Established residential infrastructure and access to everyday conveniences.\n\n      **Watch out for:** The exact unit price may use up more of your budget, depending on size, floor and condition.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n</box>\n\n## What I would choose\n\n| Your priority | My pick |\n|---|---|\n| Best overall everyday family life | **Nimbus The Hyde Park** |\n| Commute and metro convenience | **Sethi Max Royal** |\n| Staying in Greater Noida West | **Arihant Abode** |\n| Another Sector 78 option | **Sunshine Helios** |\n\nI would put **Elite X on hold for now**. Since you need a home within two years, I would not choose an under-construction project over a suitable completed flat simply for its newer amenities or advertised open-space percentage.\n\n## The next step\n\nVisit The Hyde Park and Sethi Max Royal on the same day. See the actual 3BHKs, check the common areas, speak to two or three residents, and drive from each society to your office in Sector 63 during peak traffic.\n\nOne thing will help me narrow the choice further: **which matters more to you?**\n\n{@body const [priority,setPriority] = DIL.useState(\"space\")}\n{@body const [condition,setCondition] = DIL.useState(\"ready\")}\n<box border radius=\"xl\" padding={3} gap={4}>\n  <text weight=\"medium\">1. What matters most at home?</text>\n  <radio-group value={priority} onChange={setPriority} direction=\"col\">\n    <radio value=\"space\">Large open spaces, parks and children's play areas</radio>\n    <radio value=\"commute\">Shorter commute, metro and everyday convenience</radio>\n    <radio value=\"quality\">Better apartment quality and a well-maintained society</radio>\n  </radio-group>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <text weight=\"medium\">2. What condition would you prefer?</text>\n  <radio-group value={condition} onChange={setCondition} direction=\"col\">\n    <radio value=\"ready\">Ready to move, even if the society is a few years old</radio>\n    <radio value=\"new\">Newer construction, provided possession within two years is credible</radio>\n  </radio-group>\n  <button block onClick={() => GenUI.issueNewTurn(\"Recommend the best 3BHK society for my family in Noida based on my ₹2 crore budget, Sector 63 office, schools, shopping, and need to move within two years. Top priority: \"+({\"space\":\"large open spaces, parks and children's play areas\",\"commute\":\"shorter commute, metro and everyday convenience\",\"quality\":\"apartment quality and society maintenance\"}[priority])+\". Property preference: \"+({\"ready\":\"ready-to-move, even if the society is a few years old\",\"new\":\"newer construction with credible possession within two years\"}[condition])+\". Compare specific societies, current asking prices and risks.\")}>Find my best-fit society</button>\n</box>"
                ]
            },

                    "fallbackMarkdown": "For your everyday life, I would shortlist **Nimbus The Hyde Park in Sector 78 first, followed by Sethi Max Royal in Sector 76 and Arihant Abode in Sector 10, Greater Noida West**\\.\n\nI'm basing this on your actual priorities: a 3BHK for your family, a budget of ₹2 crore, good schools nearby, plenty of open space, shopping and daily conveniences, a reasonable commute to Sector 63, and a home you can move into within two years.\n\nThe key decision is whether you value Central Noida's established connectivity more than Greater Noida West's newer developments and potentially larger apartment for the money.\n\n## My shortlist for your everyday life\ngenui{\"map_widget_v2\":{\"points\":[{\"ref\":\"turn558351business10\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business0\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business12\"},{\"ref\":\"turn558351business4\"}],\"carousel\":{\"visibility\":\"hidden\"},\"title\":\"Societies worth visiting\"}}\n\nimage_group{\"query\":[\"IITL Nimbus The Hyde Park Sector 78 Noida landscaped central park apartment towers\"],\"aspect_ratio\":\"4:5\",\"layout\":\"carousel\",\"num_per_query\":1}\n\n## 1\\. entity[\"turn558351business10\",\"Nimbus The Hyde Park\"]\n\n(Best overall fit)\n\nSector 78 · Established society\n\nThis is my first choice for your family because the campus has landscaped open areas, children's play spaces and amenities, while Sector 78 has established shopping, schools and metro access. citeturn558351search0turn558351search8\n\n**Budget\\:** Recent listings show some 3BHK resale apartments around ₹1.6 crore, leaving more room for transaction costs than a ₹2 crore purchase. citeturn558351search0\n\n**Check carefully\\:** Residents' reviews raise concerns about seepage, interior finishes and maintenance in some apartments. Inspect the exact tower and flat rather than assuming quality is uniform.\n\n---\n\nimage_group{\"query\":[\"Sethi Max Royal Sector 76 Noida residential society landscaped courtyard towers\"],\"aspect_ratio\":\"4:5\",\"layout\":\"carousel\",\"num_per_query\":1}\n\n## 2\\. entity[\"turn558351business0\",\"Sethi Max Royal\"]\n\nSector 76 · Connectivity-focused choice\n\nA good alternative if your daily commute and access to public transport matter more than having the largest landscaped campus.\n\n**Why it fits\\:** Established gated housing, amenities and proximity to Sector 76 metro.\n\n**Watch out for\\:** Traffic around the entrance, waterlogging or seepage history, and whether the available 3BHK has enough usable space for your family.\n\n**Budget\\:** Recent resale listings have shown apartments around ₹1.6 crore, though the exact unit and total acquisition cost need confirmation. citeturn558351search0turn558351business0\n\n---\n\nimage_group{\"query\":[\"Arihant Abode Sector 10 Greater Noida West residential society landscaped green courtyard towers\"],\"aspect_ratio\":\"4:5\",\"layout\":\"carousel\",\"num_per_query\":1}\n\n## 3\\. entity[\"turn558351business12\",\"Arihant Abode\"]\n\nSector 10, Greater Noida West · Local alternative\n\nKeep this on your list if you prefer staying in Greater Noida West and want a newer-style, amenity-focused residential community.\n\n**Why it fits\\:** Family facilities, a clubhouse and a residential setting within your preferred broader area.\n\n**Watch out for\\:** Check the exact apartment's sunlight, ventilation, water quality, maintenance charges and usable area. Do not assume that a large advertised super area means a large living space.\n\n**Budget\\:** Verify the current ready-to-move resale inventory and registry eligibility before comparing it with Central Noida.\n\n---\n\nimage_group{\"query\":[\"Sunshine Helios Sector 78 Noida residential apartment society garden towers\"],\"aspect_ratio\":\"4:5\",\"layout\":\"carousel\",\"num_per_query\":1}\n\n## 4\\. entity[\"turn558351business4\",\"Sunshine Helios\"]\n\nSector 78 · Alternative to compare\n\nAdd this to your visits if you want another established society in the same general residential belt. Compare the actual 3BHK layout, light, ventilation and maintenance against The Hyde Park.\n\n**Why it fits\\:** Established residential infrastructure and access to everyday conveniences.\n\n**Watch out for\\:** The exact unit price may use up more of your budget, depending on size, floor and condition.\n\n## What I would choose\n\n| Your priority | My pick |\n|--------|--------|\n| Best overall everyday family life | **Nimbus The Hyde Park** |\n| Commute and metro convenience | **Sethi Max Royal** |\n| Staying in Greater Noida West | **Arihant Abode** |\n| Another Sector 78 option | **Sunshine Helios** |\n\nI would put **Elite X on hold for now**\\. Since you need a home within two years, I would not choose an under-construction project over a suitable completed flat simply for its newer amenities or advertised open-space percentage.\n\n## The next step\n\nVisit The Hyde Park and Sethi Max Royal on the same day. See the actual 3BHKs, check the common areas, speak to two or three residents, and drive from each society to your office in Sector 63 during peak traffic.\n\nOne thing will help me narrow the choice further: **which matters more to you?**\n\n1\\. What matters most at home?\n\nLarge open spaces, parks and children's play areas\n\nShorter commute, metro and everyday convenience\n\nBetter apartment quality and a well-maintained society\n\n---\n\n2\\. What condition would you prefer?\n\nReady to move, even if the society is a few years old\n\nNewer construction, provided possession within two years is credible\n\n[Find my best-fit society]",

                            "title": "Research Noida Real Estate Queries",

                            "pub_date": 1791965293.065,
                            "snippet": "$deep-research @Web search I want you to do deep research, search around the entire web, and tell me what the top queries are that are being asked by people around the real estate market in Noida. It can be from a user asking, \"Hey, which is the best property to put money in Noida?\" or \"What are the payment plans offered by this particular project, let's say Elite X, Sector 10 of Noida?\" There is a vast range of what the user's query might be.  I want you to give me a list of 10 different queries. &#x20;\n\n1. Give me some one-flow of the entire chat. Let's say you ask, \"Hey, I want to purchase a property in Noida,\" and then it helps you: \"Hey, Noida offers these zones, and these are the pros and cons of each zone. Do you have any particular commute or anything?\" There can be a follow-up question, and regarding that, the flow goes on for up to 4 to 5 chats.\n2. I want you to give me 5 different chats of 5 different conversations. Let's say there will be 5 runs for each chat. You start a new chat, and then you ask it 5 queries in that particular chat. Those can be related. You can change the context mid-chat, whatever you feel like, but give me the top queries you can use.\n\n&#x20; Answer the web, answer in the pub queries, and then give me the queries.",

                                "title": "UP RERA new advisory: 6 key checks homebuyers should do before purchasing property",
                                "snippet": "On May 14, 2026, the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP RERA) issued a new advisory to safeguard homebuyers from potential frauds by builders and developers. The advisory outlines six essential checks that should be completed before buying any property in Uttar Pradesh:\n\n1. Verify the land title and ownership details.\n2. Examine the sanctioned map and layout plan.\n3. Ensure the inventory details provided by the builder match official records.\n4. Check the project's bank account or collection account details.\n5. Review the project's Quarterly Progress Reports (QPRs).\n6. Investigate the number of complaints previously filed against the builder.\n\nUP RERA encourages homebuyers to use its official online portal to access verified project details and perform these checks efficiently, promoting transparency and accountability in the real estate sector.",

                                    "turn_index": 302871,
                                    "ref_type": "news",

                                    "url": "https://m.economictimes.com/wealth/real-estate/up-rera-new-advisory-6-key-checks-homebuyers-should-do-before-purchasing-property/articleshow/131093517.cms",
                                    "title": "UP RERA new advisory: 6 key checks homebuyers should do before purchasing property",
                                    "snippet": "On May 14, 2026, the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP RERA) issued a new advisory to safeguard homebuyers from potential frauds by builders and developers. The advisory outlines six essential checks that should be completed before buying any property in Uttar Pradesh:\n\n1. Verify the land title and ownership details.\n2. Examine the sanctioned map and layout plan.\n3. Ensure the inventory details provided by the builder match official records.\n4. Check the project's bank account or collection account details.\n5. Review the project's Quarterly Progress Reports (QPRs).\n6. Investigate the number of complaints previously filed against the builder.\n\nUP RERA encourages homebuyers to use its official online portal to access verified project details and perform these checks efficiently, promoting transparency and accountability in the real estate sector.",
                                    "ref_id": {
                                        "turn_index": 302871,
                                        "ref_type": "news",

        {
            "id": "51731163-f48a-414f-bf06-b037cd5c544d",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {}
            },
            "create_time": 1792070662.454556,
            "update_time": 1792070671.57909,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "Before buying a flat in Noida or Greater Noida West, I would focus on **three risks above all: whether you can legally register the property, whether the full cost is affordable, and whether you can actually take possession when promised**.\n\nUP-RERA's 2026 buyer advisory specifically recommends checking land ownership, sanctioned plans, inventory records, project bank accounts, construction progress reports and builder complaints. <Cite refs={[\"turn302871search0\"]}/>\n\n## 1. Legal risks you must check\n\n{@body const legalChecks = [\n{title:\"Ownership and title\",detail:\"Have an independent property lawyer verify the ownership chain, land rights, sale deed or sub-lease documents, and any third-party claims.\"},\n{title:\"Registry eligibility\",detail:\"Confirm in writing that the exact flat can be registered or conveyed to you, and identify any authority dues or approvals that could prevent it.\"},\n{title:\"Occupancy certificate (OC)\",detail:\"For a ready-to-move flat, verify that the OC actually covers your tower or phase. A possession letter alone is not enough.\"},\n{title:\"Approved building plan\",detail:\"Match the tower, flat number, layout, balconies, parking and promised amenities against the sanctioned plan.\"},\n{title:\"Mortgages and litigation\",detail:\"Check registered encumbrances, lender charges, court disputes, land acquisition issues and any authority restrictions affecting the property.\"},\n{title:\"RERA record\",detail:\"Check registration validity, declared completion date, quarterly progress reports, complaints and orders against the promoter.\"}\n]}\n{@body const [legalDone,setLegalDone] = DIL.useState([])}\n<box border radius=\"lg\" padding={3} gap={3}>\n  <row align=\"center\" justify=\"between\">\n    <title size=\"lg\">Legal due diligence</title>\n    <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">{legalDone.length}/6</text>\n  </row>\n  {#each legalChecks as item}\n    <checkbox key={item.title} checked={legalDone.includes(item.title)} onChange={v=>setLegalDone(old=>v?[...old,item.title]:old.filter(x=>x!==item.title))} lineThrough={false}>\n      **{item.title}**\n\n      <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">{item.detail}</text>\n    </checkbox>\n  {/each}\n</box>\n\nThe distinction between possession and ownership is particularly important. A recent Greater Noida West dispute involved buyers who had paid in full but could not register their flats because of builder dues to government agencies. <Cite refs={[\"turn302871news15\"]}/>\n\nUseful official resource: <Link url=\"https://www.up-rera.in/\" title=\"UP-RERA portal\"/>.\n\n## 2. Financial risks that can cost you lakhs\n\n| Risk | What you should do |\n|---|---|\n| Hidden charges | Get a written, all-inclusive cost sheet covering parking, club membership, power backup, maintenance deposits and other charges. |\n| GST and stamp duty | Confirm applicability and current rates with your lawyer or tax adviser. Eligible under-construction purchases generally attract GST; qualifying completed-property sales generally do not. |\n| Outstanding home loan | If the seller has a loan, obtain the lender's foreclosure statement, repayment process and release of the mortgage documents. |\n| Builder or society dues | Obtain written clearance for maintenance, electricity, water, property tax and applicable authority dues. |\n| Loan approval | Obtain a loan sanction or pre-approval, but remember that bank approval does not guarantee a clean title. |\n| Resale liquidity | Compare recent transactions and how long similar flats take to sell. An expensive flat in a poorly maintained society can be difficult to exit. |\n| Monthly running costs | Calculate EMI, maintenance, property tax, insurance, repairs and furnishing expenses before committing. |\n\n**For your ₹2 crore budget, keep the purchase price below the maximum you can afford all-in.** Do not exhaust your savings on the flat and leave nothing for registration, interiors, emergencies or loan payments.\n\n## 3. Extra risks if you buy under construction\n\nIf you are considering Elite X or another under-construction project, check these additional points:\n\n- The latest construction progress against the registered completion deadline.\n- Whether the promoter has uploaded recent RERA progress reports.\n- The payment schedule and whether each instalment corresponds to a verifiable milestone.\n- The agreement's possession date, delay compensation, cancellation terms and refund conditions.\n- Whether the land or project is mortgaged and, if so, how the lender's rights affect your purchase.\n- Whether the promoter has adequate funding to finish construction.\n\nRERA registration alone does not guarantee delivery on time. <Cite refs={[\"turn302871search0\",\"turn302871search1\"]}/>\n\n## 4. Red flags that should make you stop\n\n<box border radius=\"lg\" padding={3} gap={2}>\n  {#each [\"The seller or builder refuses to share original documents or the complete cost sheet.\",\"You are told to pay a large token immediately to secure a discount.\",\"The flat cannot be registered, or registry eligibility is unclear.\",\"The advertised area does not match the agreement or sanctioned plan.\",\"The builder promises possession before the registered deadline without supporting evidence.\",\"There are unresolved title disputes, unexplained mortgages or unpaid authority dues.\",\"The seller asks you to transfer money to a personal account instead of the documented recipient.\"] as warning}\n    <row align=\"start\" gap={2}>\n      <icon name=\"alert-triangle\" color=\"danger\" size=\"lg\"/>\n      <box flex=\"1\">{warning}</box>\n    </row>\n  {/each}\n</box>\n\n## 5. My recommendation for you\n\nBecause you need a home within two years, I would prioritise a **ready-to-move 3BHK with verified registry eligibility, a valid occupancy certificate and a clean title**. Have an independent property lawyer review the documents before you pay a non-refundable token.\n\nIf you share the **society name, flat price and whether it is a resale or builder sale**, I can help you prepare a transaction-specific checklist of the documents and financial risks to investigate before you commit."
                ]
            },

                                "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/noida/strange-that-homebuyers-who-have-paid-in-full-cant-register-flats-because-of-govt-builder-dispute-hc/articleshow/134950768.cms",
                                "title": "Strange that homebuyers who have paid in full can't register flats because of govt-builder dispute: Allahabad high court",
                                "snippet": "The Allahabad High Court has expressed concern over the situation where fully paid homebuyers are unable to register their flats due to a dispute between the builder and government agencies. The issue was highlighted in a plea filed by residents of Ajnara Homes in Greater Noida West. The court found it \"strange\" that buyers who fulfilled their financial obligations are being penalized because the real estate developer has failed to clear its dues to government bodies. In response, the court has directed the state government, Greater Noida Industrial Development Authority, and the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP-RERA) to provide a clear plan of action to address this issue. The case underscores the continuing problems faced by homebuyers in India due to regulatory and developer-related delays.",
                                "ref_id": {
                                    "turn_index": 302871,
                                    "ref_type": "news",
                                    "ref_index": 15
                                },

                    {
                        "type": "search_result_group",
                        "domain": "www.financialexpress.com",
                        "entries": [
                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://www.financialexpress.com/money/sale-deed-not-registered-after-buying-a-flat-what-happens-to-your-ownership-rights-4360430/",
                                "title": "Sale deed not registered after buying a flat? What happens to your ownership rights?",
                                "snippet": "Buying a flat without registering the sale deed can leave the buyer in a precarious legal position under Indian law. Despite paying in full, receiving possession, and moving in, the buyer does not gain legal ownership until the sale deed is officially registered. An unregistered sale deed allows the seller—or their legal heirs in case of death—to retain legal ownership, which can create complications if disputes arise or the property is resold. Simply having an agreement of sale gives the right to buy but does not transfer ownership. A signed but unregistered sale deed leaves property title incomplete. However, the buyer can seek legal recourse; for example, by involving the Sub-Registrar of Assurances to summon the seller for registering the deed. Indian law allows up to four months to register the deed from the date of execution, extendable up to eight months with valid justification. Failing to register the deed means the buyer’s rights are not protected, posing significant legal and financial risks. Legal experts suggest that in case of the seller’s death, the buyer can enforce the transaction against the seller’s legal heirs. Proper legal procedures and timely registration are crucial to ensure full ownership rights.",

                                "title": "UP RERA new advisory: 6 key checks homebuyers should do before purchasing property",
                                "snippet": "On May 14, 2026, the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP RERA) issued a new advisory to safeguard homebuyers from potential frauds by builders and developers. The advisory outlines six essential checks that should be completed before buying any property in Uttar Pradesh:\n\n1. Verify the land title and ownership details.\n2. Examine the sanctioned map and layout plan.\n3. Ensure the inventory details provided by the builder match official records.\n4. Check the project's bank account or collection account details.\n5. Review the project's Quarterly Progress Reports (QPRs).\n6. Investigate the number of complaints previously filed against the builder.\n\nUP RERA encourages homebuyers to use its official online portal to access verified project details and perform these checks efficiently, promoting transparency and accountability in the real estate sector.",

                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/noida/strange-that-homebuyers-who-have-paid-in-full-cant-register-flats-because-of-govt-builder-dispute-hc/articleshow/134950768.cms",
                                "title": "Strange that homebuyers who have paid in full can't register flats because of govt-builder dispute: Allahabad high court",
                                "snippet": "The Allahabad High Court has expressed concern over the situation where fully paid homebuyers are unable to register their flats due to a dispute between the builder and government agencies. The issue was highlighted in a plea filed by residents of Ajnara Homes in Greater Noida West. The court found it \"strange\" that buyers who fulfilled their financial obligations are being penalized because the real estate developer has failed to clear its dues to government bodies. In response, the court has directed the state government, Greater Noida Industrial Development Authority, and the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP-RERA) to provide a clear plan of action to address this issue. The case underscores the continuing problems faced by homebuyers in India due to regulatory and developer-related delays.",
                                "ref_id": {
                                    "turn_index": 225497,
                                    "ref_type": "news",
                                    "ref_index": 7
                                },
                                "pub_date": 1792005921.0,
                                "thumbnail_url": "https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-1/bphWeFu_QakZV4DXZXdCXPT24TuY2jLbf5yTbPZTBGbHpTTYBayGhRgesRe-VWllXLjP1co-rrUaXOmm_ZsaBEjgoSy_76Nl9MzmT7wb3eg1bqYvoWqH-oebJDLmXi6_LuRccKM4Xzsi2Qy8eF20f2z_QdnLipQuJ2bKnWRssj69zDl9JEc0A8PGgexZhz58",
                                "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"

                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/real-estate/news/ncrs-housing-market-getting-pricier-bigger/articleshow/134868173.cms",
                                "title": "'NCR's housing market getting pricier & bigger': Report",
                                "snippet": "A recent report by PropEquity titled \"NCR Real Estate 360 degrees - From wealth creation to developer consolidation\" highlights significant growth in the housing market of the National Capital Region (NCR), specifically in areas like Gurugram and Noida. Over the past decade, the typical homebuyer preference has shifted from 2 BHK homes to larger 3 BHK units of nearly 2,000 sq ft. Discounts that were previously available have largely disappeared, indicating a stronger and more competitive market. The total sales value in NCR reached approximately Rs 1.6 lakh crore in 2025, reflecting a 1.7-fold increase from under Rs 1 lakh crore in 2023. Among the NCR localities, Gurugram has emerged as the dominant driver, contributing about 60% of the total sales value. Meanwhile, Mumbai continues to lead the national housing market with a sales value around Rs 2 lakh crore.",
                                "ref_id": {
                                    "turn_index": 225497,
                                    "ref_type": "news",
                                    "ref_index": 8
                                },
                                "pub_date": 1791712105.0,

                                "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"
                            }
                        ]
                    },
                    {
                        "type": "search_result_group",
                        "domain": "navbharattimes.indiatimes.com",
                        "entries": [
                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://navbharattimes.indiatimes.com/real-estate/ncr-housing-sales-fall-11-percent-diwali-home-buying-guide/articleshow/134822330.cms",
                                "title": "NCR में घरों की बिक्री में 11% की बड़ी गिरावट, क्या Diwali पर घर खरीदने का यह सही समय, फ्लैट होंगे सस्ते?",
                                "snippet": "जनवरी से सितंबर 2026 के दौरान दिल्ली-NCR में घरों की बिक्री में 11% की गिरावट दर्ज की गई है, जिससे कुल बिक्री 35,574 यूनिट्स पर आ गई। नाइट फ्रैंक इंडिया की रिपोर्ट के अनुसार, इस गिरावट की मुख्य वजह डेवलपर्स द्वारा उच्च कीमत वाले फ्लैट्स बनाना है, जिनकी कीमत 2 करोड़ रुपये से अधिक है। किफायती और मिड-सेगमेंट फ्लैट्स की कमी के चलते मध्यम वर्ग के खरीदारों की पहुंच कठिन हुई है। इस स्थिति के कारण बाजार में बिना बिके फ्लैट्स की संख्या भी अधिक है, जिससे सप्लाई ज्यादा और डिमांड कम हो रही है। ऐसे में प्रॉपर्टी की कीमतों में गिरावट की संभावना जताई जा रही है, जिससे त्योहारों के मौसम में, विशेष रूप से दिवाली के दौरान घर खरीदना खरीदारों के लिए अधिक फायदेमंद हो सकता है। यह स्थिति निवेशकों के लिए निर्णय लेने का एक महत्वपूर्ण समय हो सकता है कि वर्तमान में घर खरीदें या कीमतों के और गिरने का इंतजार करें।",
                                "ref_id": {

                                    "ref_type": "news",

                                "title": "Noida home prices soar 125%, Gurugram 117% since 2019; check property rates and rental yields across 11 cities",
                                "snippet": "Between 2019 and Q2 2026, property prices in Noida surged by 125%, increasing from ₹4,795 per square foot to ₹10,780.                                 "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/delhi/noida-flat-delay-homebuyer-compensation-allahabad-hc-rera-10903712/",
                                "title": "'Paid Rs 40,000 rent, EMI for years': Noida buyer wins compensation for delayed flat",
                                "snippet": "Shyam Sunder Agrawal, a government official from Delhi, booked a 3BHK flat in April 2010 at Antriksh Golf View in Noida's Sector 78 for Rs 43 lakh, with possession promised by 2012. Seeking proximity to his office and a nearby school for his young child, he intended it to be a permanent family home. However, the flat was only handed over in March 2017, more than five years after the promised date. During the prolonged delay, Agrawal was forced to rent a house in Vasant Kunj, incurring monthly costs of Rs 40,000, while also managing EMI payments. The delay severely disrupted his initial plans and financial stability, turning what was meant to be a fresh beginning for his young family into a drawn-out struggle. Eventually, he pursued legal action and won compensation for the delayed possession, highlighting ongoing challenges faced by homebuyers dealing with real estate project delays.",

                                    "snippet": "जनवरी से सितंबर 2026 के दौरान दिल्ली-NCR में घरों की बिक्री में 11% की गिरावट दर्ज की गई है, जिससे कुल बिक्री 35,574 यूनिट्स पर आ गई। नाइट फ्रैंक इंडिया की रिपोर्ट के अनुसार, इस गिरावट की मुख्य वजह डेवलपर्स द्वारा उच्च कीमत वाले फ्लैट्स बनाना है, जिनकी कीमत 2 करोड़ रुपये से अधिक है। किफायती और मिड-सेगमेंट फ्लैट्स की कमी के चलते मध्यम वर्ग के खरीदारों की पहुंच कठिन हुई है। इस स्थिति के कारण बाजार में बिना बिके फ्लैट्स की संख्या भी अधिक है, जिससे सप्लाई ज्यादा और डिमांड कम हो रही है। ऐसे में प्रॉपर्टी की कीमतों में गिरावट की संभावना जताई जा रही है, जिससे त्योहारों के मौसम में, विशेष रूप से दिवाली के दौरान घर खरीदना खरीदारों के लिए अधिक फायदेमंद हो सकता है। यह स्थिति निवेशकों के लिए निर्णय लेने का एक महत्वपूर्ण समय हो सकता है कि वर्तमान में घर खरीदें या कीमतों के और गिरने का इंतजार करें।",
                                    "ref_id": {
                                        "turn_index": 225497,
                                        "ref_type": "news",
                                        "ref_index": 9
                                    },

                                    "url": "https://m.economictimes.com/wealth/real-estate/noida-home-prices-soar-125-gurugram-117-since-2019-check-property-rates-and-rental-yields-across-11-cities/articleshow/132856445.cms",
                                    "title": "Noida home prices soar 125%, Gurugram 117% since 2019; check property rates and rental yields across 11 cities",
                                    "snippet": "Between 2019 and Q2 2026, property prices in Noida surged by 125%, increasing from ₹4,795 per square foot to ₹10,780. Gurugram also experienced a significant rise of 117% in the same period. Rental yields in Noida improved modestly from 3.2% in 2019 to 3.9% in Q2 2026. The report further highlights trends in property prices and rent increases across 11 Indian cities, including notable markets like Delhi NCR, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, and Mumbai.                                 {
                                    "type": "search_result",
                                    "url": "https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/delhi/noida-flat-delay-homebuyer-compensation-allahabad-hc-rera-10903712/",
                                    "title": "'Paid Rs 40,000 rent, EMI for years': Noida buyer wins compensation for delayed flat",
                                    "snippet": "Shyam Sunder Agrawal, a government official from Delhi, booked a 3BHK flat in April 2010 at Antriksh Golf View in Noida's Sector 78 for Rs 43 lakh, with possession promised by 2012. Seeking proximity to his office and a nearby school for his young child, he intended it to be a permanent family home. However, the flat was only handed over in March 2017, more than five years after the promised date. During the prolonged delay, Agrawal was forced to rent a house in Vasant Kunj, incurring monthly costs of Rs 40,000, while also managing EMI payments. The delay severely disrupted his initial plans and financial stability, turning what was meant to be a fresh beginning for his young family into a drawn-out struggle. Eventually, he pursued legal action and won compensation for the delayed possession, highlighting ongoing challenges faced by homebuyers dealing with real estate project delays.",

                                        "ref_type": "news",

                                {
                                    "type": "search_result",
                                    "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/noida/strange-that-homebuyers-who-have-paid-in-full-cant-register-flats-because-of-govt-builder-dispute-hc/articleshow/134950768.cms",
                                    "title": "Strange that homebuyers who have paid in full can't register flats because of govt-builder dispute: Allahabad high court",
                                    "snippet": "The Allahabad High Court has expressed concern over the situation where fully paid homebuyers are unable to register their flats due to a dispute between the builder and government agencies. The issue was highlighted in a plea filed by residents of Ajnara Homes in Greater Noida West. The court found it \"strange\" that buyers who fulfilled their financial obligations are being penalized because the real estate developer has failed to clear its dues to government bodies. In response, the court has directed the state government, Greater Noida Industrial Development Authority, and the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP-RERA) to provide a clear plan of action to address this issue. The case underscores the continuing problems faced by homebuyers in India due to regulatory and developer-related delays.",
                                    "ref_id": {

                                    "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"

                                {
                                    "type": "search_result",
                                    "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/real-estate/news/ncrs-housing-market-getting-pricier-bigger/articleshow/134868173.cms",
                                    "title": "'NCR's housing market getting pricier & bigger': Report",
                                    "snippet": "A recent report by PropEquity titled \"NCR Real Estate 360 degrees - From wealth creation to developer consolidation\" highlights significant growth in the housing market of the National Capital Region (NCR), specifically in areas like Gurugram and Noida. Over the past decade, the typical homebuyer preference has shifted from 2 BHK homes to larger 3 BHK units of nearly 2,000 sq ft. Discounts that were previously available have largely disappeared, indicating a stronger and more competitive market. The total sales value in NCR reached approximately Rs 1.6 lakh crore in 2025, reflecting a 1.7-fold increase from under Rs 1 lakh crore in 2023. Among the NCR localities, Gurugram has emerged as the dominant driver, contributing about 60% of the total sales value. Meanwhile, Mumbai continues to lead the national housing market with a sales value around Rs 2 lakh crore.",

                                    "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"
                                }

                                    "type": "search_result",
                                    "url": "https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/delhi/noida-flat-delay-homebuyer-compensation-allahabad-hc-rera-10903712/",
                                    "title": "'Paid Rs 40,000 rent, EMI for years': Noida buyer wins compensation for delayed flat",
                                    "snippet": "Shyam Sunder Agrawal, a government official from Delhi, booked a 3BHK flat in April 2010 at Antriksh Golf View in Noida's Sector 78 for Rs 43 lakh, with possession promised by 2012. Seeking proximity to his office and a nearby school for his young child, he intended it to be a permanent family home. However, the flat was only handed over in March 2017, more than five years after the promised date. During the prolonged delay, Agrawal was forced to rent a house in Vasant Kunj, incurring monthly costs of Rs 40,000, while also managing EMI payments. The delay severely disrupted his initial plans and financial stability, turning what was meant to be a fresh beginning for his young family into a drawn-out struggle. Eventually, he pursued legal action and won compensation for the delayed possession, highlighting ongoing challenges faced by homebuyers dealing with real estate project delays.",

        {
            "id": "23e3975f-ccbb-47a1-ac86-bedb0415dde4",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {}
            },
            "create_time": 1792070702.335879,
            "update_time": 1792070712.116728,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "**My recommendation: don't rush to buy, but don't wait indefinitely for property prices to fall either. Start looking now and buy when you find the right flat at the right price.**\n\nGiven your situation, I'd lean towards buying a **ready-to-move 3BHK within the next 3–6 months**, provided the finances and legal checks work out.\n\nYou have two constraints that matter more than trying to predict the property market:\n\n- You want a home within two years.\n- Your budget is around ₹2 crore, and you want a good society with open space, nearby schools and shopping, and a reasonable commute to Sector 63.\n\nWaiting can help you compare more properties, save for the purchase, and negotiate with sellers. But waiting solely because you expect prices to fall is a gamble: desirable properties may remain expensive, and mortgage rates, rents and your own housing needs can change.\n\n## Why I wouldn't wait purely for lower prices\n\nRecent market signals are mixed.\n\n- **Prices have risen substantially over the longer term.** Noida residential prices were reported to have increased 125% between 2019 and Q2 2026. <Cite refs={[\"turn225497news10\"]}/>\n- **Sales have cooled recently.** A Knight Frank report cited by Navbharat Times reported an 11% year-on-year decline in Delhi-NCR housing sales during January–September 2026, suggesting buyers may have more negotiating room in some segments. <Cite refs={[\"turn225497news9\"]}/>\n- **Prices vary widely by project.** Greater Noida West listings show substantial differences between ready-to-move apartments and premium new launches. Asking prices are not the same as registered transaction prices. <Cite refs={[\"turn225497search3\",\"turn225497search4\"]}/>\n\nMy interpretation: this could be a reasonable time to negotiate, but the evidence doesn't establish that a broad price correction is imminent.\n\n## A simple decision rule\n\n<box border radius=\"lg\" padding={3} gap={3}>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <icon name=\"check-circle\" color=\"success\" size=\"xl\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      **Buy now if…**\n\n      You find a legally clear, ready-to-move 3BHK in a good society, the all-in cost fits your budget, and you expect to live there for at least 5–7 years.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <icon name=\"clock\" size=\"xl\" color=\"secondary\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      **Wait a few months if…**\n\n      Sellers are quoting inflated prices, you haven't compared enough societies, or buying now would leave you with little emergency savings. Use the time to negotiate and compare completed properties.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n  <divider color=\"subtle\"/>\n  <row align=\"start\" gap={3}>\n    <icon name=\"shield-alert\" color=\"danger\" size=\"xl\"/>\n    <box flex=\"1\" gap={1}>\n      **Don't buy yet if…**\n\n      The property has unclear registry eligibility, unresolved title issues, questionable construction quality or a payment plan that stretches your finances.\n    </box>\n  </row>\n</box>\n\n## What I would do in your position\n\nI would spend the next **4–8 weeks** comparing at least five ready-to-move 3BHKs across Sectors 76–78 and 120, plus one or two options in Greater Noida West. Negotiate based on comparable properties, keep registration and interior costs within your ₹2 crore budget, and buy only after legal verification.\n\nI would not wait a year hoping prices fall by 10–15%. There is no reliable evidence that such a decline will happen, and a good, fairly priced property may be worth securing earlier.\n\nOne thing could change my advice considerably: **will you be taking a home loan, or paying mostly from your own funds?** That affects how much waiting, negotiating and financial risk makes sense."
                ]
            },
            "status": "finished_successfully",

                    "fallbackMarkdown": "**My recommendation: don't rush to buy, but don't wait indefinitely for property prices to fall either. Start looking now and buy when you find the right flat at the right price.**\n\nGiven your situation, I'd lean towards buying a **ready-to-move 3BHK within the next 3–6 months**, provided the finances and legal checks work out.\n\nYou have two constraints that matter more than trying to predict the property market\\:\n\n- You want a home within two years.\n- Your budget is around ₹2 crore, and you want a good society with open space, nearby schools and shopping, and a reasonable commute to Sector 63.\n\nWaiting can help you compare more properties, save for the purchase, and negotiate with sellers. But waiting solely because you expect prices to fall is a gamble: desirable properties may remain expensive, and mortgage rates, rents and your own housing needs can change.\n\n## Why I wouldn't wait purely for lower prices\n\nRecent market signals are mixed.\n\n- **Prices have risen substantially over the longer term.** Noida residential prices were reported to have increased 125% between 2019 and Q2 2026. citeturn225497news10\n- **Sales have cooled recently.** A Knight Frank report cited by Navbharat Times reported an 11% year-on-year decline in Delhi-NCR housing sales during January–September 2026, suggesting buyers may have more negotiating room in some segments. citeturn225497news9\n- **Prices vary widely by project.** Greater Noida West listings show substantial differences between ready-to-move apartments and premium new launches. Asking prices are not the same as registered transaction prices. citeturn225497search3turn225497search4\n\nMy interpretation: this could be a reasonable time to negotiate, but the evidence doesn't establish that a broad price correction is imminent.\n\n## A simple decision rule\n\n**Buy now if…**\n\nYou find a legally clear, ready-to-move 3BHK in a good society, the all-in cost fits your budget, and you expect to live there for at least 5–7 years.\n\n---\n\n**Wait a few months if…**\n\nSellers are quoting inflated prices, you haven't compared enough societies, or buying now would leave you with little emergency savings. Use the time to negotiate and compare completed properties.\n\n---\n\n**Don't buy yet if…**\n\nThe property has unclear registry eligibility, unresolved title issues, questionable construction quality or a payment plan that stretches your finances.\n\n## What I would do in your position\n\nI would spend the next **4–8 weeks** comparing at least five ready-to-move 3BHKs across Sectors 76–78 and 120, plus one or two options in Greater Noida West. Negotiate based on comparable properties, keep registration and interior costs within your ₹2 crore budget, and buy only after legal verification.\n\nI would not wait a year hoping prices fall by 10–15%. There is no reliable evidence that such a decline will happen, and a good, fairly priced property may be worth securing earlier.\n\nOne thing could change my advice considerably: **will you be taking a home loan, or paying mostly from your own funds?** That affects how much waiting, negotiating and financial risk makes sense.",

                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/noida/strange-that-homebuyers-who-have-paid-in-full-cant-register-flats-because-of-govt-builder-dispute-hc/articleshow/134950768.cms",
                                "title": "Strange that homebuyers who have paid in full can't register flats because of govt-builder dispute: Allahabad high court",
                                "snippet": "The Allahabad High Court has expressed concern over the situation where fully paid homebuyers are unable to register their flats due to a dispute between the builder and government agencies. The issue was highlighted in a plea filed by residents of Ajnara Homes in Greater Noida West. The court found it \"strange\" that buyers who fulfilled their financial obligations are being penalized because the real estate developer has failed to clear its dues to government bodies. In response, the court has directed the state government, Greater Noida Industrial Development Authority, and the Uttar Pradesh Real Estate Regulatory Authority (UP-RERA) to provide a clear plan of action to address this issue. The case underscores the continuing problems faced by homebuyers in India due to regulatory and developer-related delays.",
                                "ref_id": {
                                    "turn_index": 225497,
                                    "ref_type": "news",
                                    "ref_index": 7
                                },
                                "pub_date": 1792005921.0,
                                "thumbnail_url": "https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-1/bphWeFu_QakZV4DXZXdCXPT24TuY2jLbf5yTbPZTBGbHpTTYBayGhRgesRe-VWllXLjP1co-rrUaXOmm_ZsaBEjgoSy_76Nl9MzmT7wb3eg1bqYvoWqH-oebJDLmXi6_LuRccKM4Xzsi2Qy8eF20f2z_QdnLipQuJ2bKnWRssj69zDl9JEc0A8PGgexZhz58",
                                "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"
                            },
                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/real-estate/news/ncrs-housing-market-getting-pricier-bigger/articleshow/134868173.cms",
                                "title": "'NCR's housing market getting pricier & bigger': Report",
                                "snippet": "A recent report by PropEquity titled \"NCR Real Estate 360 degrees - From wealth creation to developer consolidation\" highlights significant growth in the housing market of the National Capital Region (NCR), specifically in areas like Gurugram and Noida. Over the past decade, the typical homebuyer preference has shifted from 2 BHK homes to larger 3 BHK units of nearly 2,000 sq ft. Discounts that were previously available have largely disappeared, indicating a stronger and more competitive market. The total sales value in NCR reached approximately Rs 1.6 lakh crore in 2025, reflecting a 1.7-fold increase from under Rs 1 lakh crore in 2023. Among the NCR localities, Gurugram has emerged as the dominant driver, contributing about 60% of the total sales value. Meanwhile, Mumbai continues to lead the national housing market with a sales value around Rs 2 lakh crore.",
                                "ref_id": {
                                    "turn_index": 225497,
                                    "ref_type": "news",
                                    "ref_index": 8
                                },
                                "pub_date": 1791712105.0,
                                "thumbnail_url": "https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/photo/134868229.cms?imgsize=216426&cfs=8JL5MBsDYFNo",
                                "attribution": "timesofindia.indiatimes.com"
                            }
                        ]
                    },
                    {
                        "type": "search_result_group",
                        "domain": "navbharattimes.indiatimes.com",
                        "entries": [
                            {
                                "type": "search_result",
                                "url": "https://navbharattimes.indiatimes.com/real-estate/ncr-housing-sales-fall-11-percent-diwali-home-buying-guide/articleshow/134822330.cms",
                                "title": "NCR में घरों की बिक्री में 11% की बड़ी गिरावट, क्या Diwali पर घर खरीदने का यह सही समय, फ्लैट होंगे सस्ते?",
                                "snippet": "जनवरी से सितंबर 2026 के दौरान दिल्ली-NCR में घरों की बिक्री में 11% की गिरावट दर्ज की गई है, जिससे कुल बिक्री 35,574 यूनिट्स पर आ गई। नाइट फ्रैंक इंडिया की रिपोर्ट के अनुसार, इस गिरावट की मुख्य वजह डेवलपर्स द्वारा उच्च कीमत वाले फ्लैट्स बनाना है, जिनकी कीमत 2 करोड़ रुपये से अधिक है। किफायती और मिड-सेगमेंट फ्लैट्स की कमी के चलते मध्यम वर्ग के खरीदारों की पहुंच कठिन हुई है। इस स्थिति के कारण बाजार में बिना बिके फ्लैट्स की संख्या भी अधिक है, जिससे सप्लाई ज्यादा और डिमांड कम हो रही है। ऐसे में प्रॉपर्टी की कीमतों में गिरावट की संभावना जताई जा रही है, जिससे त्योहारों के मौसम में, विशेष रूप से दिवाली के दौरान घर खरीदना खरीदारों के लिए अधिक फायदेमंद हो सकता है। यह स्थिति निवेशकों के लिए निर्णय लेने का एक महत्वपूर्ण समय हो सकता है कि वर्तमान में घर खरीदें या कीमतों के और गिरने का इंतजार करें।",

                                "title": "Noida home prices soar 125%, Gurugram 117% since 2019; check property rates and rental yields across 11 cities",
                                "snippet": "Between 2019 and Q2 2026, property prices in Noida surged by 125%, increasing from ₹4,795 per square foot to ₹10,780.                                 "snippet": "Shyam Sunder Agrawal, a government official from Delhi, booked a 3BHK flat in April 2010 at Antriksh Golf View in Noida's Sector 78 for Rs 43 lakh, with possession promised by 2012. Seeking proximity to his office and a nearby school for his young child, he intended it to be a permanent family home. However, the flat was only handed over in March 2017, more than five years after the promised date. During the prolonged delay, Agrawal was forced to rent a house in Vasant Kunj, incurring monthly costs of Rs 40,000, while also managing EMI payments. The delay severely disrupted his initial plans and financial stability, turning what was meant to be a fresh beginning for his young family into a drawn-out struggle. Eventually, he pursued legal action and won compensation for the delayed possession, highlighting ongoing challenges faced by homebuyers dealing with real estate project delays.",
